The constitution gives the council three mandates: veto power over legislation passed by the [parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majlis_of_Iran),[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] supervision of elections[^cite_note-IDP-239] and approving or disqualifying candidates seeking to run in local, parliamentary, presidential, or Assembly of Experts elections.[^cite_note-Article_99_of_the_constitution-240] The council can nullify a law based on two accounts: being against [sharia](28840.md) (Islamic law), or being against the constitution.[^cite_note-Article_4_Archived_9_December_2006_at_the_Wayback_Machine-241]&#x20;

### Supreme National Security Council

Main article: [Supreme National Security Council](6295948.md)

The Supreme National Security Council (SNSC) is at the top of the foreign policy decisions process.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] The council was formed during the [1989 Iranian constitutional referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_Iranian_constitutional_referendum) for the protection and support of national interests, the revolution, territorial integrity and national sovereignty.[^cite_note-245] It is mandated by Article 176 of the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran) to be presided over by the president.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247]&#x20;

The leader selects the secretary of the Supreme Council, and the council's decisions are effective after the leader confirms them. The SNSC formulates nuclear policy and would become effective if the Leader confirms them.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249]&#x20;

### Legislature

Main article: [Legislature of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature_of_Iran)

See also: [Specialized Commissions of the Parliament of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specialized_Commissions_of_the_Parliament_of_Iran), [Supreme Audit Court of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Audit_Court_of_Iran), and [Majlis Research Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majlis_Research_Centre)



&#x20;The building of the [Islamic Consultative Assembly](383276.md) (ICA)

The legislature, known as the [Islamic Consultative Assembly](383276.md), Iranian Parliament or "Majles", is a [unicameral](204304.md) body comprising 290 members elected for four years.[^cite_note-Majlis-250] It drafts legislation, ratifies international treaties, and approves the national budget. All parliamentary candidates and legislation from the assembly must be approved by the Guardian Council.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] The Guardian Council can dismiss elected members of the parliament.[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254] The parliament has no legal status without the Guardian Council, and the Council holds absolute veto power over legislation.[^cite_note-Archived_copy-255]&#x20;

The [Expediency Discernment Council](452730.md) has the authority to mediate disputes between Parliament and the Guardian Council. It serves as an advisory body to the Supreme Leader, making it one of Iran's most powerful governing bodies.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-Article_4_Archived_9_December_2006_at_the_Wayback_Machine-241] The Parliament has 207 constituencies, including the 5 [reserved seats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserved_seats) for religious minorities. The remaining 202 are territorial, covering one or more of Iran's counties.&#x20;

### Law

Main article: [Judicial system of the Islamic Republic of Iran](66501437.md)

Iran uses the sharia law (based on [Ja'fari school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ja%27fari_school)) as its legal system, with elements of [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)). The Supreme Leader appoints the head of the Supreme Court and chief public prosecutor. There are several types of courts, including public courts that deal with civil and criminal cases, and [revolutionary courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolutionary_Court), which deal with certain offenses, such as crimes against [national security](240468.md). The decisions of the revolutionary courts are final and cannot be appealed.&#x20;

The Chief Justice heads the judicial system and is responsible for its administration and supervision. He is the highest judge of the [Supreme Court of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Iran). The Chief Justice nominates candidates to serve as minister of justice, and the president selects one. The Chief Justice can serve for two five-year terms.[^cite_note-257]&#x20;

The [Special Clerical Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Clerical_Court) handles crimes allegedly committed by [clerics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleric), although it has taken on cases involving [laypeople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laity). The Special Clerical Court functions independently of the regular judicial framework and is accountable only to the Rahbar. The Court's rulings are final and cannot be appealed.[^cite_note-Judiciary-234] The Assembly of Experts, which meets for one week annually, comprises 86 "virtuous and learned" clerics elected by adult suffrage for 8-year terms.&#x20;

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Administrative divisions of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Iran), [Regions of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Iran), [Provinces of Iran](448160.md), [Counties of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Iran), and [List of cities in Iran by province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Iran_by_province)

Iran operates as a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state)[^cite_note-Unitary_1-258][^cite_note-Unitary_2-259] and is subdivided into thirty-one [provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province) ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): استان, *ostân*), each governed from a local center, usually the largest local city, which is called the capital (Persian: مرکز, *[markaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markaz_\(country_subdivision\))*) of that province. The provincial authority is headed by a [governor-general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-general) (استاندار, *ostândâr*), who is appointed by the [Minister of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(Iran\)) subject to the approval of [the cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Iran).[^cite_note-govgen-260]&#x20;

A map of Iran's provinces

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Iran](64709.md)



Nations with which Iran has diplomatic relations

Iran maintains diplomatic relations with 165 countries, but not the United States and Israel—a state which Iran derecognized in 1979.[^cite_note-MousavianShahidsaless2014-261] Iran has an [adversarial relationship with Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_conflict) due to different political ideologies. [Iran and Turkey](8026795.md) have been involved in modern proxy conflicts such as in [Syria](7515849.md), [Libya](17633.md), and the [South Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Caucasus).[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] However, they have shared common interests, such as the issue of [Kurdish separatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurdish_nationalism) and the [Qatar diplomatic crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar_diplomatic_crisis).[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] Iran has a close and strong relationship with [Tajikistan](30108.md).[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270] Iran also has a strong relationship with [Pakistan](7090083.md) sharing common interests, such as the issue of [Baloch separatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baloch_separatism).[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

Iran formed the [Axis of Resistance](36658251.md), a coalition of actors committed to countering the influence of the United States and Israel in the region.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2024-272][^cite_note-AlKassab-2023-273] It most notably includes the [Hezbollah](13919.md) in Lebanon, [Islamic Resistance](75274618.md) and [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md) in Iraq, and the [Houthi movement](17240332.md) in Yemen.[^cite_note-274] It sometimes[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] includes [Hamas](13913.md)[^cite_note-277] and a variety of [other Palestinian militant groups](2646043.md).[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] Iran has deep economic relations and alliance with Iraq, Lebanon, and did with Syria, which was described as Iran's "closest ally".[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] In December 2024, the [fall of the Assad regime](78548066.md) in Syria, a close ally of Iran, was a severe setback for the political influence of Iran in the region.[^cite_note-283]&#x20;



The building of the [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](1166971.md), which extensively uses [Achaemenid architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_architecture) in its facade, [National Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Garden,_Tehran)

Russia is a key trading partner, especially in regard to its excess oil reserves.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] Both share a close economic and military alliance and are subject to heavy sanctions by Western nations.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] Iran is the only country in Western Asia that has been invited to join the [CSTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_Security_Treaty_Organization), the Russia-based international treaty organization that parallels [NATO](21133.md).[^cite_note-290]&#x20;

Relations between Iran and China are strong economically; they have developed a friendly, economic and strategic relationship. In 2021, Iran and China signed a [25-year cooperation agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93China_25-year_Cooperation_Program) that will strengthen the relations between the two countries and would include "political, strategic and economic" components.[^cite_note-291] [Iran-China relations](29416684.md) dates back to at least 200 BC and possibly earlier.[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] Iran is one of the few countries in the world that has a good relationship with both [North](21255.md) and [South Korea](27019.md).[^cite_note-294] Iran is a member of dozens of international organizations, including the [G-15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_15), [G-24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_24), [G-77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_77), [IAEA](14984.md), [IBRD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bank_for_Reconstruction_and_Development), [IDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Development_Association), [NAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement), [IDB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Development_Bank), [IFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Finance_Corporation), [ILO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Labour_Organization), [IMF](15251.md), [IMO](14986.md), [Interpol](14338129.md), [OIC](47725.md), [OPEC](166346.md), [WHO](33583.md), and the [UN](31769.md), and currently has observer status at the [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization).&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Military history of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Iran)



[MRBM](478272.md) [Sejjil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sejjil). Iran is the world's 6th [missile power](38025.md), and the 5th country in the world with [hypersonic missile technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypersonic_missile)

The military is organized under a unified structure, the [Islamic Republic of Iran Armed Forces](14305018.md), comprising the [Islamic Republic of Iran Army](48259216.md), which includes the [Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Iran_Army_Ground_Forces), [Air Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Iran_Air_Defense_Force), [Air Force](412792.md), and [Navy](2160969.md); the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md), which consists of the [Ground Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps_Ground_Forces), [Aerospace Force](5613577.md), [Navy](4676345.md), [Quds Force](4028355.md), and [Basij](2892082.md); and the [Police Command](5187924.md) (Faraja), which serves an analogous function to a [gendarme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendarmerie). While the IRIAF protects the country's sovereignty in a traditional capacity, the IRGC is mandated to ensure the integrity of the Republic against foreign interference, coups, and internal riots.[^cite_note-295] In 2019, the United States under [President Donald Trump](4848272.md) officially labeled the Revolutionary Guard as a foreign terrorist organization. It is the first time that an element of a foreign state was designated as a terrorist organization.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] [Since 1925](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_Iran), all male citizens aged 18 must serve around 14 months in the IRIAF or IRGC.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]&#x20;

Iran has over 610,000 active troops and around 350,000 reservists, which is at least over one million military personnel, one of the world's highest [percentage of citizens with military training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] The Basij, a [paramilitary](146730.md) volunteer militia within the IRGC, has over 20 million members, 600,000 available for immediate call-up, 300,000 reservists, and one million that could be mobilized when necessary.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] Faraja, the Iranian uniformed [police force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_force), has over 260,000 active personnel. Most statistical organizations do not include the Basij and Faraja in their rating reports.&#x20;

Iran ranks 7th in the number of active military personnel[^cite_note-307] and 9th in size of both its ground force and armoured force. Iran's armed forces are the largest in West Asia and comprise the greatest [Army Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_Aviation) fleet in the Middle East.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] Iran is among the top 15 countries in terms of military budget.[^cite_note-311] In 2021, its military spending increased for the first time in four years, to \$24.6 billion, 2.3% of the national GDP.[^cite_note-312] Funding for the IRGC accounted for 34% of Iran's total military spending in 2021.[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

Since the Islamic revolution, to overcome foreign embargoes, Iran has developed a domestic military industry capable of producing indigenous [tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tank), [armoured personnel carriers](19278686.md), [missiles](19594.md), [submarines](28825.md), [missile destroyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_frigate_Jamaran), [radar](25676.md) systems, [helicopters](8286923.md), [naval vessels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy), and [fighter planes](10929.md).[^cite_note-AskariMohseni2010-314] Official announcements have highlighted the development of advanced weaponry, particularly in rocketry.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] Consequently, Iran has the largest and most diverse [ballistic missile](38025.md) arsenal in the Middle East and is only the 5th country in the world with [hypersonic missile technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypersonic_missile).[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] Iran designs and produces a variety of [unmanned aerial vehicles](58900.md).[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] It is one of the world's five countries with [cyberwarfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberwarfare) capabilities and is identified as "one of the most active players in the international cyber arena".[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]&#x20;

Following Russia's purchase of Iranian drones during the [Russo-Ukrainian war](70149799.md),[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327] in November 2023, the IRIAF finalized arrangements to acquire Russian [Sukhoi Su-35](314292.md) fighter jets, [Mil Mi-28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mil_Mi-28) attack helicopters, air defense and missile systems.[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329] The Iranian Navy has had joint exercises with Russia and China.[^cite_note-330]&#x20;

### Nuclear program

Main article: [Nuclear program of Iran](721807.md)

Iran's nuclear program dates back to the 1950s.[^cite_note-331] In the early 1970s, the Shah revived Iran's nuclear energy ambitions as he claimed that the country "needed alternative sources of fuel for the day when the oil reserves would be depleted."[^cite_note-332] Iran expanded its nuclear program after the Islamic Revolution, and its extensive nuclear fuel cycle, including enrichment capabilities, became the [subject of intense international negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Views_on_the_nuclear_program_of_Iran) and [economic sanctions](25699056.md).[^cite_note-333] Many countries have expressed concern Iran could divert civilian nuclear technology into a [weapons program](1757165.md).[^cite_note-334] In 2015, Iran and the [P5+1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P5%2B1) agreed to the [Joint Comprehensive Plan on Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action), which [aimed to end economic sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1929) in exchange for restriction in producing [enriched uranium](37555.md).[^cite_note-335]&#x20;

In 2018, however, the [US withdrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_withdrawal_from_the_Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action) from the deal under the [Trump administration](52231341.md) and reimposed sanctions. This was met with resistance by Iran and other members of the P5+1.[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338] A year later, Iran began decreasing its compliance.[^cite_note-jcpoafp-339] By 2020, Iran announced it would no longer observe any limit set by the agreement.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] Progress since then has brought Iran to the [nuclear threshold status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_latency).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-jcpoafp-339] As of November 2023, Iran had uranium enriched to up to 60% fissile content, close to weapon grade.[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347] Some analysts already regard Iran as a *[de facto](43594.md)* nuclear power.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349]&#x20;

### Regional influence

Main articles: [Iranian influence in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_influence_in_Lebanon), [Iranian intervention in the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_the_Syrian_civil_war), and [Iranian intervention in Iraq (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%93present\))

Iran's significant influence and foothold was sometimes characterized as the "Dawn of A New Persian Empire".[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Some analysts associated the Iranian influence to the nation's proud [national legacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_nationalism), [empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_Empire) and [history](64213.md).[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]&#x20;

After the revolution, Iran grew its influence across and beyond the region.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] It built military forces with a wide network of state and none-state actors, starting with Hezbollah in Lebanon in 1982.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362] The IRGC was key to Iranian influence, through its Quds Force.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365] The instability in Lebanon (from the 1980s),[^cite_note-366] Iraq (from 2003)[^cite_note-367] and Yemen (from 2014)[^cite_note-368] allowed Iran to build strong alliances and footholds beyond its borders. Iran had a prominent influence in the social services, education, economy and politics of Lebanon,[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] and Lebanon provides Iran access to the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md).[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] Hezbollah's strategic successes against Israel, such as its symbolic victory during the [2006 Israel–Hezbollah War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), elevated Iran's influence in the [Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant) and strengthened its appeal across the [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world).[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374]&#x20;

After the [US invasion of Iraq in 2003](201936.md) and the arrival of [ISIS](9087364.md) in the mid-2010s, Iran financed and trained militia groups in Iraq.[^cite_note-Institute_for_the_Study_of_War-375][^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] After the Iran-Iraq war and the fall of [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein), Iran shaped Iraq's politics.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] Following Iraq's struggle against ISIS in 2014, companies linked to the IRGC such as [Khatam al-Anbiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khatam_al-Anbiya_Construction_Headquarters), started to build roads, power plants, hotels and businesses in Iraq, creating an economic corridor worth around \$9 billion before [COVID-19](63030231.md).[^cite_note-381]&#x20;



Achaemenid Empire in 500 BC

During [Yemen's civil war](46215853.md) Iran provided military support to the Houthis,[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] a [Zaydi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaydism) Shia movement fighting Yemen's [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam) government since 2004,[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386] which gained [significant power](75207766.md).[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389] Iran has considerable influence in Afghanistan through militant groups such as [Fatemiyoun Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_Fatemiyoun)[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] and [Hezbollah Afghanistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_Afghanistan)[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-aijac-393] and also in Pakistan through militant groups such as [Zainabiyoun Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_Zainabiyoun)[^cite_note-394] and [Sipah-e-Muhammad Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sipah-e-Muhammad_Pakistan).[^cite_note-pakistantoday.com.pk-395]&#x20;

In Syria, Iran supported President [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md);[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] the two countries were long-standing allies.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-Institute_for_the_Study_of_War-375] Iran provided significant military and economic support to Assad's government,[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] so had a considerable foothold in Syria.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] Iran has long supported organizations in [North Africa](21714.md) in countries like [Algeria](358.md) and [Tunisia](30188.md). Iran embraced [Hamas](13913.md) in part to help undermine the popularity of the [Palestinian Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) (PLO).[^cite_note-403] Iran's support of Hamas emerged more clearly in later years.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] According to US intelligence, Iran does not have full control over these state and non-state groups.[^cite_note-408]&#x20;

### Censorship

Main articles: [Censorship in Iran](4297092.md) and [Political repression in the Islamic Republic of Iran](80259436.md)

Censorship is ranked among the most extreme worldwide.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411] Iran has strict [internet censorship](12831280.md), with the government persistently blocking social media and other sites.[^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413][^cite_note-TechCrunch-414] Since January 2021, authorities have blocked several social media platforms.[^cite_note-415]&#x20;

The 2006 election results were widely disputed, resulting in [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Iranian_presidential_election_protests).[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] The [2017–18 Iranian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_Iranian_protests) swept across the country in response to the economic and political situation.[^cite_note-420] It was formally confirmed that thousands of protesters were arrested.[^cite_note-421] The [2019–20 Iranian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Iranian_protests) started on 15 November in [Ahvaz](599079.md), and spread across the country after the government announced increases in fuel prices of up to 300%.[^cite_note-422] A [week-long total Internet shutdown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Internet_blackout_in_Iran) marked one of the most severe Internet blackouts in any country, and the bloodiest governmental crackdown of the protestors.[^cite_note-423] Tens of thousands were arrested and hundreds were killed within a few days according to multiple international observers, including [Amnesty International](18947898.md).[^cite_note-424]&#x20;

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in Iran](16652449.md)



The entrance to [Evin Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evin_Prison), established in 1972. [VICE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_Media) describes the prison as the "legendary terrifying place that nobody wants to end up."

[^cite_note-425]

The Iranian government has been denounced by various international organizations and governments for violating human rights.[^cite_note-426] The government has frequently persecuted and arrested critics of the government. Iranian law does not recognise [sexual orientations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Iran). Sexual activity between members of the same sex is illegal and is [punishable by death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_penalty_for_homosexuality).[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428] [Capital punishment](5902.md) is a legal punishment, and Iran is reported to have the highest execution rate per capita in the world.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] UN Special Rapporteur [Javaid Rehman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javaid_Rehman) has reported discrimination against several ethnic minorities in Iran.[^cite_note-432] A group of UN experts in 2022 urged Iran to stop "systematic persecution" of religious minorities, adding that members of the [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith) were arrested, barred from universities, or had their homes demolished.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434] In 2024, a UN Special Rapporteur investigation concluded that Iran committed [genocide](12441.md) against its political and religious minorities during [1981–1982 massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981%E2%80%931982_Iran_Massacres) and [1988 mass executions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_executions_of_Iranian_political_prisoners).[^cite_note-ShahinClerical-435]&#x20;

[Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_International_Airlines_Flight_752) was a scheduled international civilian passenger flight from Tehran to [Kyiv](585629.md), operated by [Ukraine International Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_International_Airlines). On 8 January 2020, the Boeing 737–800 flying the route was shot down by the IRGC shortly after takeoff, killing all 176 occupants on board and leading to [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_International_Airlines_Flight_752_protests). An international investigation led to the government admitting to the shootdown, calling it a "human error".[^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437] Further [protests against the government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahsa_Amini_protests) began on 16 September 2022 after a woman named [Mahsa Amini died in police custody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Mahsa_Amini) following her arrest by the [Guidance Patrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guidance_Patrol), known commonly as the "morality police".[^cite_note-438][^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] Government crackdown on the protests have been described by the UN to be [crimes against humanity](61099.md).[^cite_note-442]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Iran](64704.md)

See also: [Subsidies in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidies_in_Iran) and [Banking and insurance in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_and_insurance_in_Iran)

As of 2024, Iran has the world's [19th largest economy by PPP-adjusted GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)). It is a [mixture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy) of [central planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy), [state ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_ownership) of oil and other large enterprises, village agriculture, and small-scale private trading and service ventures.[^cite_note-443] Services contribute the largest percentage of GDP, followed by industry ([mining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_in_Iran) and manufacturing) and [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Iran).[^cite_note-444] The economy is characterized by its hydrocarbon sector, manufacturing and financial services.[^cite_note-445] With 10% of the world's [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%27s_oil_reserves) and 15% of [gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%27s_Natural_Gas_Reserves), Iran is an [energy superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_superpower). Over [40 industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran) are directly involved in the [Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange).&#x20;



[Tehran Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Stock_Exchange)

Tehran is the economic powerhouse of Iran.[^cite_note-446] About 30% of Iran's public-sector workforce and 45% of its large industrial firms are located there, and half those firms' employees work for government.[^cite_note-447] The [Central Bank of Iran](3961876.md) is responsible for developing and maintaining the currency: the [Iranian rial](708656.md). The government does not recognise [trade unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_trade_unions_in_Iran) other than the [Islamic labour councils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_labor_law), which are subject to the approval of employers and the security services.[^cite_note-ayatoil-448] Iran was the first country to introduce a [national basic income](28310828.md) in Autumn 2010.[^cite_note-449] Unemployment was 9% in 2022.[^cite_note-450]&#x20;

[Budget deficits](64702.md#Budget) have been a chronic problem, mostly due to [large state subsidies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidies_in_Iran), that include foodstuffs and especially petrol, totaling \$100 billion in 2022 for energy alone.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452] In 2010, the economic reform plan was to cut subsidies gradually and replace them with targeted social assistance. The objective is to move towards free market prices and increase productivity and social justice.[^cite_note-453] The administration continues reform, and indicates it will diversify the oil-reliant economy. Iran has developed a [biotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biotechnology_in_Iran), [nanotechnology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemistry_in_Iran), and [pharmaceutical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals_in_Iran) industry.[^cite_note-454] The government is [privatising industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatization_in_Iran).&#x20;

Iran has leading manufacturing industries in automobile manufacture, transportation, construction materials, home appliances, food and agricultural goods, armaments, pharmaceuticals, information technology, and petrochemicals in the Middle East.[^cite_note-Economy-455] It is among the world's top five producers of [apricots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apricot), [cherries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry), [cucumbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cucumber) and [gherkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gherkin), [dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Date_palm#Description), [figs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_fig), [pistachios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pistachio), [quinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quince), [walnuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walnut), [Kiwifruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiwifruit) and [watermelons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watermelon).[^cite_note-456] [International sanctions against Iran](25699056.md) have damaged the economy.[^cite_note-everend-457] Iran is one of three countries that has not ratified the [Paris Agreement](30242372.md) to limit [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_the_Middle_East_and_North_Africa), although academics say it would be good for the country.[^cite_note-458]&#x20;

Iran suffers from high inflation[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] and especially [surging food prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_inflation_in_Iran). A major contributing factor is the involvement of the IRGC in the economy,[^cite_note-461] particularly in the agriculture and food sectors but also due to significant spending on the Axis of Resistance which increase the public deficit.[^cite_note-462]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Iran)



Around 12 million tourists visit [Kish Island](847277.md) annually.

[^cite_note-463]

Tourism had been rapidly growing before the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), reaching nearly nine million foreign visitors in 2019, the world's third fastest-growing tourism destination.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] In 2022 it expanded its share to 5% of the economy.[^cite_note-466] Iran's tourism experienced a growth of 43% in 2023, attracting six million foreign tourists.[^cite_note-467] The government ended visa requirements for 60 countries in 2023.[^cite_note-468]&#x20;

98% of visits are for leisure, while 2% are for business, indicating the country's appeal as a tourist destination.[^cite_note-469] Alongside the capital, the most popular tourist destinations are [Isfahan](223149.md), [Shiraz](39622.md) and [Mashhad](406512.md).[^cite_note-470] Travellers from other West Asian countries grew 31% in the first seven months of 2023.[^cite_note-471] Domestic tourism is one of the world's largest; Iranian tourists spent \$33 billion in 2021.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-BYI-473][^cite_note-MACooper2012-474] Iran projects investment of \$32 billion in the tourism sector by 2026.[^cite_note-475]&#x20;

### Agriculture and fishery

Main article: [Agriculture in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Iran)



A [paddy field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddy_field) in [Bandpey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandpey), northern Iran

Roughly one-third of Iran's surface area is suitable for farmland. Only 12% of the total land area is under cultivation, and less than one-third of the cultivated area is irrigated. The rest is devoted to [dryland farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dryland_farming). Some 92% of agricultural products depend on water.[^cite_note-476] The western and northwestern portions of the country have the most fertile soils. Iran's [food security](216361.md) index stands at around 96 percent.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478]&#x20;

3% of Iran's land area is used for grazing and fodder production. Most of the grazing is done on mostly semi-dry rangeland in mountain areas and on areas surrounding the large deserts of central Iran. Progressive government efforts and incentives during the 1990s improved agricultural productivity, helping Iran toward its goal of reestablishing national self-sufficiency in food production.&#x20;

Access to the Caspian Sea, the Persian Gulf, the Gulf of Oman, and many river basins provides the potential to develop excellent fisheries. The government assumed control of commercial fishing in 1952. Expansion of the fishery infrastructure enabled Iran to harvest an estimated 700,000 tons of fish annually from the southern waters. Since the revolution, increased attention has been focused on producing fish from inland waters. Between 1976 and 2004, the combined take from inland waters by the state and private sectors increased from 1,100 tons to 110,175 tons.[^cite_note-loc3-479] Iran is the world's largest producer and exporter of [caviar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caviar), exporting more than 300 tonnes annually.[^cite_note-news.bbc.co.uk2-480][^cite_note-481]&#x20;

### Industry and services

Main article: [Industry of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industry_of_Iran)



Iran is the world's 16th largest car manufacturer, with [IKCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Khodro) being the largest in the [Middle East](19323.md), [Central Asia](6742.md) and [North Africa](21714.md).

[^cite_note-482]

In 2024, Iran was ranked 16th in global car manufacturing, ahead of the [UK](31717.md), [Italy](14532.md), and Russia.[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484] It built 1.188 million cars in 2023, a 12% growth compared to the previous years. Iran exports cars to countries such as Venezuela, Russia and Belarus. From 2008 to 2009, Iran leaped to 28th place from 69th in annual industrial production growth rate.[^cite_note-485] Iranian [contractors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_contractor) have been awarded several foreign tender contracts in different fields of construction of [dams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_reservoirs_and_dams_in_Iran), [bridges, roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Iran), [buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_in_Iran), [railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Republic_of_Iran_Railways), [power generation](26980995.md), and [gas, oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Oil_Company) and [petrochemical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Petrochemical_Company) industries. In 2011, some 66 Iranian industrial companies were carrying out projects in 27 countries.[^cite_note-486] Iran exported over \$20 billion worth of technical and engineering services over 2001–2011. The availability of local raw materials, [rich mineral reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_in_Iran), experienced manpower have all played crucial role in winning the bids.[^cite_note-487]&#x20;

45% of large industrial firms are located in Tehran, and almost half of their workers work for government.[^cite_note-488] The Iranian retail industry is largely in the hands of cooperatives, many of them government-sponsored, and of independent retailers in the [bazaars](556904.md). The bulk of food sales occur at street markets, where the Chief Statistics Bureau sets the prices.[^cite_note-489]&#x20;



Modern [café](370815.md) in Tehran

Iran's automotive industry is the second most active industry of Iran, after its oil and gas industry. [ITMCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Tractor_Manufacturing_Company) is the biggest tractor manufacturer in the Middle East. Iran is the [12th largest automaker in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_motor_vehicle_production). Construction is one of the most important sectors in Iran. The annual [turnover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenue) in the construction industry amounts to US\$38.4 billion.[^cite_note-austrade.gov.au-490] The [Central Bank of Iran](3961876.md) indicates that 70 percent of the Iranians own homes, with huge amounts of idle money entering the housing market.[^cite_note-iran-daily1-491][^cite_note-iran-daily2-492]&#x20;

Iran is ranked among 15 major mineral-rich countries.[^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494] Iran has become self-sufficient in designing, building and operating dams and power plants. Iran is one of the six countries in the world that manufacture gas- and steam-powered turbines.[^cite_note-495]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in Iran](10580575.md)

See also: [Petroleum industry in Iran](28205267.md)



[South Pars Gas-Condensate field](9037100.md), the world's largest [gas field](2910801.md), holds 8% of Earth's gas reserves.

[^cite_note-496]

Iran is an [energy superpower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_superpower), and petroleum plays a key part.[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498] As of 2023, Iran produced 4% of the world's [crude oil](23195.md) (3.6 million barrels (570,000 m<sup>3</sup>) per day),[^cite_note-499] which generates US\$36bn[^cite_note-u048-500] of export revenue and is the main source of [foreign currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_reserves).[^cite_note-501] Oil and gas reserves are estimated at 1.2 trn barrels;[^cite_note-502] Iran holds 10% of world oil reserves and 15% for gas. Iran ranks 3rd in [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves)[^cite_note-503] and is [OPEC](166346.md)'s 2nd largest exporter. Iran has the 2nd largest [gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Iran),[^cite_note-The_Wall_Street_Journalgas-504] and [3rd largest natural gas production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_production). In 2019, Iran discovered a southern oil field of 50 bn barrels.[^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508] In April 2024, the [NIOC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_Oil_Company) discovered 10 giant shale oil deposits, totaling 2.6 billion barrels.[^cite_note-509][^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511]&#x20;

Iran manufactures 60–70% of its industrial equipment domestically, including turbines, pumps, catalysts, [refineries](195137.md), [oil tankers](15923603.md), [drilling rigs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drilling_rig), [offshore platforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_platform), towers, pipes, and exploration instruments.[^cite_note-512] The addition of new [hydroelectric](381399.md) stations and streamlining of conventional coal and oil-fired stations increased installed capacity to 33 GW; about 75% was based on natural gas, 18% on oil, and 7% on hydroelectric power. In 2004, Iran opened its first wind-powered and geothermal plants, and the first [solar](13690575.md) thermal plant began in 2009. Iran is the world's third country to develop [GTL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_to_liquids) technology.[^cite_note-513]&#x20;

[Demographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Iran) trends and intensified industrialization have caused electric power demand to grow by 8% per year. The government's goal of 53 GW of installed capacity by 2010 is to be reached by bringing on line [new gas-fired plants](26980995.md), and adding hydropower and nuclear generation capacity. [Iran's first nuclear power plant](18522794.md) went online in 2011.[^cite_note-nuclear-514][^cite_note-MüllerMüller2015-515] Despite being an energy superpower, as of 2024, [Iran suffers from an energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_energy_crisis), manifested by many power outages.[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Iran)



Students of [Amirkabir University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amirkabir_University_of_Technology) in Tehran

Iran has made considerable advances in science and technology, despite international sanctions. In the biomedical sciences, the [Institute of Biochemistry and Biophysics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Biochemistry_and_Biophysics) has a [UNESCO](21786641.md) chair in biology.[^cite_note-518] In 2006, [Iranian scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_contemporary_Iranian_scientists,_scholars,_and_engineers) successfully [cloned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloning) a sheep at the Royan Research Centre in Tehran.[^cite_note-519] [Stem cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stem_cell) research is among the top 10 in the world.[^cite_note-520] Iran ranks 15th in the world in [nanotechnologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanotechnology).[^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523] Iranian scientists outside Iran have made major scientific contributions. In 1960, [Ali Javan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Javan) co-invented the first [gas laser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_laser), and [fuzzy set](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuzzy_set) theory was introduced by [Lotfi A. Zadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotfi_A._Zadeh).[^cite_note-524]&#x20;

Cardiologist [Tofy Mussivand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tofy_Mussivand) invented and developed the first artificial cardiac pump, the precursor of the [artificial heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_heart). Furthering research in diabetes, the [HbA1c](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glycated_hemoglobin) was discovered by [Samuel Rahbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Rahbar). Many papers in [string theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/String_theory) are published in Iran.[^cite_note-Nasr2007-525] In 2014, Iranian mathematician [Maryam Mirzakhani](6409655.md) became the first woman, and Iranian, to receive the [Fields Medal](10859.md), the highest prize in mathematics.[^cite_note-526]&#x20;

Iran increased its publication output nearly tenfold from 1996 through 2004, and ranked first in output growth rate, followed by China.[^cite_note-527] According to a study by [SCImago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SCImago_Journal_Rank) in 2012, Iran would rank fourth in research output by 2018, if the trend persisted.[^cite_note-SCImago_December_2012c-528] The Iranian humanoid robot [Sorena 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surena_\(robot\)), which was designed by engineers at the [University of Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Tehran), was unveiled in 2010. The [Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Electrical_and_Electronics_Engineers) (IEEE) has placed the name of Surena among the five most prominent robots, after analysing its performance.[^cite_note-529]&#x20;

According to a 2019 study by Isabel Campos-Varela et al., Iran recorded the highest proportion of retracted publications globally, with 15.52 retractions per 10,000 publications. Media reports from 2023 indicate that Iran continues to rank among the countries with the highest retraction rates. Common types of misconduct include fraudulent peer review processes, plagiarism, and data fabrication. Data from the Retraction Watch Leaderboard further highlights that two of the 32 individuals with the highest number of retracted articles worldwide are based in Iran.[^cite_note-530] Iran was ranked 70th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-531][^cite_note-532]&#x20;

#### Iranian Space Agency

Main article: [Iranian Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Space_Agency)



Historic launch of [Safir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safir_\(rocket\)) in 2012

The [Iranian Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Space_Agency) was established in 2004. Iran became an orbital-launch-capable nation in 2009[^cite_note-533] and is a founding member of the [United Nations Committee on the Peaceful Uses of Outer Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Committee_on_the_Peaceful_Uses_of_Outer_Space). Iran placed its domestically built satellite [Omid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omid) into orbit on the 30th anniversary of the revolution, in 2009,[^cite_note-HarveySmid2011-534] through its first expendable launch vehicle [Safir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safir_\(rocket\)). It became the [9th country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_first_orbital_launches_by_country) capable of both producing a satellite and sending it into space from a domestically made launcher.[^cite_note-Hvac-conference.ir_November_29_2015c-535] [Simorgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simorgh_\(rocket\))'s launch in 2016, is the successor of Safir.[^cite_note-536]&#x20;

In January 2024, Iran launched the [Soraya satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soraya_\(satellite\)) into its highest orbit yet (750 km),[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538] a space launch milestone for the country.[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-540] It was launched by [Qaem 100 rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaem_100).[^cite_note-541][^cite_note-542] Iran also successfully launched 3 indigenous satellites, The Mahda, Kayan and Hatef,[^cite_note-543] into orbit using the Simorgh carrier rocket.[^cite_note-544][^cite_note-545] It was the first time in the country's history that it simultaneously sent three satellites into space.[^cite_note-546][^cite_note-547] The three satellites are designed for testing advanced satellite subsystems, space-based positioning technology, and narrowband communication.[^cite_note-548]&#x20;

In February 2024, Iran launched its domestically developed imaging satellite, Pars 1, from Russia into orbit.[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-550] This was the second time since August 2022, when Russia launched another Iranian remote-sensing, [Khayyam satellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khayyam_satellite), into orbit from [Kazakhstan](16642.md), reflecting deep scientific cooperation between the countries.[^cite_note-551][^cite_note-552]&#x20;

### Telecommunication

Main article: [Communications in Iran](64706.md)

See also: [Telecommunication Company of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication_Company_of_Iran) and [Iran Electronics Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Electronics_Industries)

The telecommunications industry is almost entirely state-owned, dominated by the [Telecommunication Company of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication_Company_of_Iran). As of 2020, 70 million Iranians use high-speed mobile internet. Iran is among the first five countries which have had a growth rate of over 20 percent and the highest level of development in telecommunication.[^cite_note-553] Iran has been awarded the UNESCO special certificate for providing telecommunication services to rural areas.&#x20;

Globally, Iran ranks 75th in mobile [internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet) speed and 153rd in fixed internet speed.[^cite_note-554]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Iran)





Population of Iranian provinces and counties in 2021

Iran's population grew rapidly from about 19 million in 1956 to about 85 million by February 2023.[^cite_note-555] Iran's [fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) has dropped dramatically, from 6.5 children born per woman to about 1.7 two decades later,[^cite_note-556][^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558] leading to a population growth rate of about 1.39% in 2018.[^cite_note-559] Due to its young population, studies project that the growth will continue to slow until it stabilises at around 105 million by 2050.[^cite_note-bureau-560][^cite_note-payvand-561][^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563]&#x20;

Iran hosts one of the largest refugee populations, with almost one million,[^cite_note-564] mostly from Afghanistan and Iraq.[^cite_note-565] As of 2025, Iran was home to an estimated four million [Afghan migrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghans_in_Iran) and refugees.[^cite_note-566] According to the Iranian Constitution, the government is required to provide every citizen with access to social security, covering retirement, unemployment, old age, disability, accidents, calamities, health and medical treatment and care services.[^cite_note-567] This is covered by [tax revenues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Iran) and income derived from public contributions.[^cite_note-568]&#x20;

Iran has one of the highest urban growth rates in the world. From 1950 to 2002, the urban proportion of the population increased from 27% to 60%.[^cite_note-payvand2-569] Iran's population is concentrated in its western half, especially in the north, north-west and west.[^cite_note-570] Tehran, with a population of around 9.4 million, is Iran's capital and largest city. Iran's second most populous city, [Mashhad](406512.md), has a population of around 3.4 million, and is capital of the province of [Razavi Khorasan](1289401.md). Isfahan has a population of around 2.2 million and is Iran's third most populous city. It is the capital of [Isfahan province](448061.md) and was the third capital of the Safavid Empire.&#x20;
